# Skill system parity log

Ports are listed as `Ref location -> src location`. A row is not a claim that the whole skill system is done.

## WP10.1 effect coverage audit — 2026-10-28

- `tools/audit_effect_coverage.py` reads the 693 Ref rows from `generated/effect_handler_index.tsv`, classifies current routes from `CRagEffect::ProcessEffectFrame`, `OnProcess2`, `ProcessReferenceStrOnly`, `Init`, the generated STR table and `kProvenClipWaves`, scans launch sites, writes `generated/EFFECT_COVERAGE.tsv`, and updates `generated/PARITY_AUDIT.txt`. Registered as CTest `effect_coverage_audit`.
- The first committed-baseline run reports 340 explicit routes (340 tagged `ported` under the audit's parity-log presence rule), 56 `clip-wave`, 45 `str-default`, 252 `unported`, and 112 launched-but-unported IDs. This is within ±5 for route/string/clip/unported counts versus the review probe (341/45/56/251). The 300–373 dispatch range is restricted to actual current `OnProcess2` case labels, as required by WP10.1. The launched count is ten above the probe because this scanner searches the complete `src/` implementation, including nested effect launches and generated skill-effect sources; `EFFECT_COVERAGE.tsv` names each source per ID for review. These are static route/inventory counts, not parity or runtime acceptance.

- Monster sources (`CSession::IsMonster`: object type not 9, job greater than 1000 and less than 4000) call `GetMonsterSkillInfo` through `GetJobName` and `SetAction(8 * act - 8, 1, loopType == 0)`, matching the skill-notify tail. The local player does not take that action.
- `CGameMode::Zc_Notify_Groundskill`, `Ref/GameModePacket.cpp:18135` -> `HandleGroundSkillNotify`. The 5-int block is skill, start time, cell X, cell Y, level, and it calls message 38. Non-monsters then fire the skill-47 caster arrow shower, or play `effect\가스펠.wav` (362) and `effect\바실리카.wav` (369). Those two names are the `Zc_Notify_Groundskill` rows in `playwave_callsites.tsv`.
- Meteor Storm skill83 follows the `CGameActor::Am_Groundskill` special branch (`Ref/GameActorMsgHandler.cpp:336-399`): set `m_curAction=88`, set `m_curMotion` to motion-count(88)-1, then enter the shared ground-effect switch without the generic facing or `SetState` path; case83 places effect92 at the packet cell. `meteor_storm83.tsv` replays raw packetver23 0x0117 through the production router and verifies `m_curAction=88` and effect92 at cell(10,20), including terrain height. The packet carries the cell selected/emitted by the server; this fixture does not emulate server-side random cell selection. Focused replay passes; full Release suite passes92/92.
- `CGameMode::Zc_State_Change` / `Zc_State_Change3`, `Ref/GameModePacket.cpp:26882` and `:26926` -> `HandleActorStateChange`. Message 43 receives `{body, effect, health, pk}`. Cart UI stays. Sight still launches effect 601.

`GameModePacket.cpp` compiled. `ro_gamemode` still stops on `DrawOutlinedTextQtToOverlay` in `GameMode.cpp`. Normal attack packets still use the combat queues. Skill damage no longer does.

- `CGameMode::Zc_useskill_ack`, `Ref/GameModePacket.cpp:33843` -> `HandleSkillCastAck`. `GetSkillActionInfo2` supplies the begin effect and motion. Skills 57 and 62 freeze that motion until `castTime + now` and do not send messages 85 or 82. Every other skill sets the motion, sends message 85 (`castTime >> 4`) and message 82 (`castTime`). A monster caster then applies `GetMonsterSkillInfo`; skill 193 sets `m_motionSpeed` to `castTime / 300`. A living target gets message 86. Message 81 is skipped for a mercenary, homunculus, or object type 9 when `SKILL_INFO::IsGoodSkillForActor` is true and the map is not PK or siege. Skill 490 launches effect 539 and aims it at the target. Skills 261, 401, and 1015 call `SetForceAnimation(96, 0, 0, 96, 0, 0, 0, 0)`; 401 also launches effect 402. Skill 268 calls `SetForceAnimation(96, 0, 0, 96, 0, 1, castTime + 1000, 0)`.
- `CGameMode::Zc_Use_Skill`, `Ref/GameModePacket.cpp:27959-30133` -> `HandleSkillNoDamageNotify` plus generated `ApplyUseSkillEffects`. Heal 28's source ranges are `<200 → effect7`, `[200,2000) → effect312`, `[2000,4000) → effect313`, and `>=4000 → effect325`; every branch also sends message88 with the received amount, color `-16711936`, kind114. Raw 15-byte 0x011A replays at 199/200/2000/4000 verify effect7/312/313/325 and the exact message88 payload. Ref comparator basis: the skill28 branch routes to shared label `L_158937_0` in the generated Ref-shaped switch; its nested thresholds and output order are retained verbatim. Focused four-case replay passes; full Release suite passes91/91.
- `SKILL_INFO::IsGoodSkillForActor`, `HighPriest.exe.c:263385` -> `src/skill/Skill.cpp`.
- `CSession::IsMonster`, `Ref/Session2.cpp:1970` -> `IsMonsterSkillActor`. Job is greater than 1000 and less than 4000, and object type is not 9. `CSession::IsMer` (`Ref/Session2.cpp:1950`) is `job > 6000 && job < 6048`.

The Lua skill-effect override, the skill-24 begin-effect rewrite, and the invented ground effect on the cast ack are gone from this handler.

- `CSkill`, `Ref/Skill.cpp:342`, `:1290`, and `:2928` -> `src/world/SkillUnit.cpp`. Message 45 stores the aid, the job, the cell, and visibility. A hidden unit stays visible when the creator is the local player or a party member in `m_partyList`. Periodic jobs relaunch when `m_launchCnt` reaches `durationTable[effectId]` minus 7, 8, or 20. Loop jobs keep one effect and add 21 to its duration. Job 127 returns 0 after 3 seconds. Message 37 builds a `WBA` (message 26, effect 49/152/52/51/583) and sends message 27. Message 119 reveals jobs 139–153. Trap models are the strings at VA `0x724F0C` through `0x724FD0`. `CWorld::GetSkillByAID`, `MakeSkill`, `RemoveSkill`, and `RemoveSkillByJob` are `Ref/GameWorld.cpp:825`, `:848`, `:665`, and `:721`.
- `CGameMode::Zc_Skill_Entry` / `Zc_Skill_Entry2` / `Zc_Skill_Disappear` / `Zc_Skill_Update`, `Ref/GameModePacket.cpp:25375`, `:25499`, `:25365`, and `:25684` -> `HandleSkillUnitSet`, `HandleSkillUnitSet2`, `HandleSkillDisappear`, and `HandleSkillUpdate`. The packet visible byte is ignored. The aid is pushed onto `m_visibleTrapList`, or removed if it was already there and the creator is not the local player. A new unit is created only when the job is at least 45. `Zc_Notify_Skill` sends message 37 to that unit when the source gid is not an actor, then message 99 when the source is not a monster.
- `CGraffiSkill`, `Ref/Skill.cpp:274`, `:655`, `:2043`, `:2165`, and `:2274` -> `src/world/GraffiSkill.cpp`. `0x01C9` with the graffiti flag sends messages 22 and 117. OnInit stamps `effect\alpabet.bmp` into a 384x384 texture. Letters are 32 wide, `?` and `!` are 16, and a space wraps when the next word would pass 512. Render draws an 18 by 18 cell patch rotated by the local player's `m_roty`. `OnProcess` stays alive until `0x0120`.
- `CGameMode::Zc_Notify_Act`, `Ref/GameModePacket.cpp:16727` and dispatch cases 138 and 737 -> `HandleActorActionNotify`. `0x008A` reads damage and left damage as signed 16-bit values; `0x02E1` reads both as 32-bit. Damage `-30000` is ignored. `attackMT` 0 becomes 1440. `attackedMT` 0 becomes 1440 unless the action is 0 or 4 (`CSession::IsAttack`, `HighPriest.exe.c:510509`). The ten ints are damage, left damage, action, target, start time, count, cell x `-1`, cell y `-1`, attackMT, attackedMT. The source actor gets message 11. The local player is `m_player` when the source id is `g_session.m_aid`; every other source is the actor whose `m_gid` matches.
- `CGameMode::Zc_Dispel`, `Ref/GameModePacket.cpp:10625` -> `HandleSkillCastCancel`. The actor gets messages 84, 87, and 83. Message 84 (`Ref/GameActor.cpp:2385`) sends message 24 to `m_beginSpellEffect`, drops that effect from `m_effectList`, and clears the pointer. Message 24 on `CRagEffect` (`Ref/RagEffect.cpp:12718`) clears the master.
- `CGameMode::Zc_Ack_Touseskill`, `Ref/GameModePacket.cpp:7006` -> `HandleSkillFailAck`. The local player's `m_isMotionFreezed` is cleared on every packet. Chat is sent only when the result byte is 0. Skill 1, cause 0, amount 1 clears `m_isReqEmotion`.
- `CGameMode::Zc_Use_Skill`, `Ref/GameModePacket.cpp:27959` and `HighPriest.exe.c:356425` -> `DeliverUseSkill` plus `ApplyUseSkillEffects` in `src/gamemode/UseSkillEffect.generated.inc`. The preamble always runs. The effect tree runs only when the result byte is not 0. Skills 249 and 8220 set state 2. A non-monster other than skill 9 sends `CGameMode` message 99 (skills 334 and 335 pass the target gid as the level argument; every other skill passes the signed amount). A monster, and skill 9 on any actor, calls `GetMonsterSkillInfo` and `SetAction`, including the local player. Skills 263, 272, 273, and 371 skip `GetSkillActionInfo`. Otherwise the motion is applied: state 7 when the actor is walking and the motion is 1, otherwise the motion itself. Skills 12, 18, 21, 70, 79, 83, 85, 89, 110, and 478 face the cell packed in the target gid. The effect tree is `tools/generate_use_skill_effect.py`. Its 48 `PlayWave` names are the `CGameMode::Zc_Use_Skill` rows in `playwave_callsites.tsv`. Heal (28, 334, and 8001) uses the unsigned amount: below 200 effect 7, below 2000 effect 312, below 4000 effect 313, otherwise 325, then message 88 with the signed amount, color `-16711936`, kind 114. Skill 61 sends message 82 with `400 *` the unclamped signed level, then state 13.

- `CGroundScope`, `Ref/Skill.cpp:296`, `:662`, `:2307`, `:2445`, and `:4` -> `src/world/GroundScope.cpp`. The only construction site is `Zc_useskill_ack` when the target gid is 0. Messages 22, 14, 115, and 117 store the caster, the cell, `castTime + now`, and `GetScopeSize(skill) + 2`. `OnProcess` keeps the object until that time. `Render` draws `effect\magic_target.tga` through `C3dGround::RenderGroundTile` (`HighPriest.exe.c` `0x4F28B0`), with the cell quad taken from `C3dGround15::GetGroundVertics`. The texture name is the string at VA `0x71C9F8`.
- Id 309 is `CRagEffect::FirstAid` (`HighPriest.exe.c:435687` and `HighPriest.exe.c:417238`). The first frame plays `_heal_effect.wav` at listener height plus 150 and launches `PP_FIRSTAID` (`0x3A`) with `effect\pikapika2.bmp`. `PrimFirstAid` (`HighPriest.exe.c:454032`, VA `0x628A50`) follows the actor, faces the camera, spins `height[8]` by 3 degrees, raises alpha by 4 through process 20, and fades it after process 100. It draws through `RenderCloud` case 1: unswapped corners, color `(150,150,250)`. A `height[9]` of 10 shifts the quad 4 units on X before the camera rotation. Id 332 is `HealTime` variant 1 and would use that same cloud case, but `aEffect_28` resolves only to the `effect\` prefix, so it stays unwired.

Frame 0 plays `apocalips_attack.wav` at listener height and launches two `PP_CHEMICAL` (`0x36`) prims of `effect\shockwave_c.bmp`. Each prim has four bands at `time + 90 * band`, distance 100, captured at the actor position before the effect's own `y -= 9`. `PrimChemical` (`HighPriest.exe.c:454268`) faces the camera, adds 18 alpha through process 10, and subtracts 15 after process 20. `RenderChemical` (`HighPriest.exe.c:462851`) draws a 3-degree wedge at half alpha in white and a 5-degree wedge at full alpha in `(130,130,255)`, both with additive blending.

Four bands use heights 100, 70, 40, and 15, spins 180, 270, 0, and 90, alphas 100, 80, 60, and 40, distances 1.5, 3, 4, and 5, and rise angles 90 through 87. `PrimTeleportation2` (`HighPriest.exe.c:451366`) spins each band by `5 - band`, pulses `maxHeight` from the stored height byte, and fills the 21 samples with `maxHeight * (1 + sin(angle) * sin(9 * sample) * 0.3)`. The scratch is the input z slot (EBP+0x10), shared across layers in this call. Mode 3 writes the same slot. Kept that behavior explicitly. `Ref/dhxj/RagEffect.cpp` shows the same old-quotient read but declares its `r` uninitialized; the 2008 binary determines the port.

## CEffectPrim::RenderEffectTexture - 2026-10-27

- Source dispatch `Ref/dhxj/RagEffectPrim.cpp:3348-3351` routes PP_EFFECTTEXTURE (0x52), PP_EFFECTTEXTURE2 (0x5F), and PP_EFFECTTEXTURE_ANI (0x80) to `RenderEffectTexture`. Current `CEffectPrim::Render` now routes all three selectors to the shared renderer.
- Branch-level comparison: source `Ref/dhxj/RagEffect2.cpp:23958-24098`, decompiled Ref `Ref/RagEffect2.cpp:13559`, canonical `HighPriest.exe.c` helper at 0x63B5A0. Readable, decompiled and canonical bodies agree on geometry and modes 0/2/3/4/10/12; the executable body also exposes mode 100. The loop draws each GI lane only for `life == 1 && alphaB > 0`. Radius is distance plus `sin(height[8])*distance*0.05`. Corner steps are 90/90/90 by default, 60/120/60 for height10=1, and 130/50/130 for height10=2. Global GI0 mode 3 offsets every corner by +3.1 X/+6 Z before screen rotation; then current lane vecB_now translates the corners. Global GI0 mode controls rendering, while the current lane supplies alpha and texture index.
- Render modes: 0/3 submit one white inverse-source-alpha quad; 2 submits blue-tinted additive then intentionally falls through to white, gray(150), and gray(50) additive quads; 4 submits the latter three; 10 submits the latter two; 12 submits gray(50); 100 submits RGB(155,50,50) inverse-source-alpha; other modes submit nothing. ## `CEffectPrim::RenderMapParticle` / PP_MAPPARTICLE (0x1B) — renderer route completed — 2026-10-27

- Canonical `HighPriest.exe.c:445654` and `Ref/RagEffect2.cpp:17538` bodies compare equal after whitespace normalization. The branch map covers the four-GI loop and `life == 1` gate; four corners at 45-degree intervals; `Tei_TowardScreen` with GI0/GI1 `alphaT`; ordered center sets B-pre, B-now, T-pre, T-now; mode-dependent alpha (150/130), fade formula below Y=-1, lower clamp, render cutoff at Y<=0, mode-dependent RGB, and four `RenderTeiRect` calls per active lane.
- The executable references eight orientation floats in `.data`: four addresses have raw zero bytes and four lie in the `.data` zero-fill tail. The four file-backed values are zero and the PE loader zero-fills the rest. `Ref/dhxj/RagEffect2.cpp:10684` instead sets `Angle = 45`; this conflicts with executable runtime values, so the implementation follows the EXE and records the discrepancy in `MISMATCHES.md`.

They use the existing camera-facing rotation and raw `ProjectVertex` equivalent; each band adds the parent's position in `RenderTeiRect` order and submits two ordered triangles through `AddRP` with source texture slot0, UVs, additive blend, and raw primitive render flags. PP_MAPPARTICLE now renders without a master and no longer falls through to the unrelated procedural radial glow.

## `CEffectPrim::RenderPeong` / PP_PEONG (0x5E) — renderer route completed — 2026-10-27

- Canonical `HighPriest.exe.c:446790` (address `0x61FAE0`) and dispatcher case `0x5E` at `:471782` define a four-lane renderer; current code had only the update path and no render route. # `0x01CD` / Zc_AutoSpellList packet and receiver state branch — 2026-10-27

- Canonical handler VA `0x5A7DC0` is 101 bytes (`HighPriest.exe` file `0x1A7DC0..0x1A7E24`); following 11 bytes are alignment padding. EXE decompilation and Ref bodies match. The handler counts nonzero 32-bit IDs at `+2,+6,...,+26`, creates WID `0x63` only when count is positive, then emits ordered message 31 calls for nonzero IDs. Dispatcher case 461 and packet length 30 agree with Ref packet_db. `tools/tests/check_autospell_packet.py` encodes the full function bytes and branch mapping.
- `UISpellListWnd::SendMsg` message 31 inserts the spell ID and default item record, then sends message 60; message 60 recalculates the scrollbar then invalidates. Executable selector table, jump targets, branch byte prefixes, EXE decompilation, Ref `UIFrameWnd2.cpp`, and the bounded source model are checked by `UISpellListWndReceiverTests.cpp`.
- Production packet-body tests cover no IDs, sparse IDs and order, window-creation failure, and malformed packets. All three focused CTests and the x64 Release build pass. The receiver is not yet wired into the window manager and does not render or synchronize inherited UI state. Visible parity remains open; this intermediate build was not deployed.

## Scope clarification (2026-10-27)

The implementation plan covers gameplay skill/spell logic and state, effects, and animations. It does not require a 1:1 recreation of legacy UI windows, layout, rendering, controls, or input. Keep the bounded guild-skill and auto-spell receiver models as optional groundwork when they do not interfere with Modern Qt UI; no legacy window factory or presentation work is required. UI-only presentation branches (including `0x0145` image display and `0x01CD` auto-spell list presentation) are excluded from gameplay acceptance. `0x0162` packet data/state behavior remains in scope. Modern Qt UI code was not changed by the UI groundwork checkpoints.

The cursor pair spawns once through `CGameMode::MakeMsgEffect(31)`, appends it to the actor's message-effect list, sends message 50 with the actor, then records `m_cursorEffect`; release sends message 53 before clearing the slot. `DeleteMatchingEffect` also clears the slot if the sprite is destroyed independently.
- Complete Ref/EXE `MakeMsgEffect` bodies match. Executable case 31 selects `cursors.spr`, `cursors.act`, process type 26, action 10; the factory initializes the position through effect message 14, whose default route offsets Y by 20. This resolved the earlier assumption that type 31 used the factory default resources.
- Message 139 forwards to `m_iceEffect`. Complete Ref/EXE `CMsgEffect::SendMsg` bodies and message switches match; the branch assigns/nulls the master actor first, then for non-null sender performs two ordered `rand()` calls (X then Z), each offsetting the actor coordinate by `(rand()%11-5)*0.1`, leaving Y unchanged. Current code preserves the order and uses double intermediate arithmetic before storing floats. Type-5 processing later follows the master position as source does; this actor-message branch comparison does not claim a persistent jittered position.
- `tools/tests/check_actor_sendmsg_cursor_ice_effect_cases.py` compares the complete source bodies and implementation routes, factory resource/process/action, cursor lifecycle, and RNG order; registered as CTest `actor_sendmsg_cursor_ice_source_check`.
- Inventory: 75 direct actor cases; no remaining in-scope source IDs without direct/inherited routes. Overall actor function coverage still excludes UI-only arms and does not establish missing `CRagEffect` handler visual parity.
- All 13 focused actor CTests pass; x64 Release builds with only existing unrelated C4244 warnings. Authorized OldRO deployment SHA-256 matches Release: `0DBD6BF2BA3442DFE08EABE4120DD0573ECF32ADAAE8F557EECE0CBFE68A1518`. No live runtime visual acceptance is claimed.

# `CGameActor::Am_Action` action and WBA scheduling (2026-10-27)

- Complete `Ref/GameActorMsgHandler.cpp` and canonical `HighPriest.exe.c` `CGameActor::Am_Action` bodies compare equal after whitespace normalization. The nested action ID sequence mechanically matches `[0,4,8,9,10,11,1,2,3]` in current source.
- Branch map: read total/left damage, action, target GID, hit count, target cell and attack/attacked motion values from `arg1[0,1,2,3,5,6,7,8,9]`; set `m_targetGid`; resolve self by session AID, otherwise actor AID then skill AID; if unresolved, mirror around local player for facing. Clamp invalid hit count to 1 and force single-hit except actions 8/9. Actions 1/2/3 set states 5/6/0 and return. Attack actions optionally launch counter effect 131 and attach it with the actor as sender, set motion factor/state/facing, and apply source job delays (+192 for 1016/1420, +912 for 1285/1830, +408 for 1286/1829/1287). Actions 4/9 select WBA message 39; action 10 selects 93 with effect1=1; action 11 selects 94 after counter reset; remaining attack actions select 26. For a found target, WBA fields preserve the source target GID, cells, message, effects, damage, level, time, and attacked-speed factor. Damage is divided per hit; extra hits advance by `doubleAttackTerm` (canonical EXE value 200), and residual damage uses the source non-player-job gate and delay `term/4 + term + term/2`.
- `tools/tests/check_am_action_source_case.py` checks complete Ref/EXE body equality, source/current case order, parameter offsets, target lookup priority and fallback, counter sender, facing formula, action map, job delays, all WBA members, and the residual/multi-hit branches. Registered as CTest `actor_am_action_source_check`.
- The implementation retains a null-input guard and `DbgLog` unknown-action diagnostic instead of source `Trace`; neither changes valid gameplay input behavior. No live packet replay or WBA timing log is claimed. All 14 focused actor CTests pass, x64 Release succeeds, `git diff --check` passes, and authorized OldRO SHA-256 matches Release: `91F17FDDD681ADFF9C373CA020D8A145D2B18A739145C037EAE7930C3CC0A950`.

# `CPc::ProcessState` animation branches (2026-10-27)

- Source anchors: `Ref/Pc.cpp:2382–2423` → consolidated current `CGameActor::ProcessState` (`src/world/GameActor.cpp:4597`, branches near 4788–4859). The canonical EXE body at `HighPriest.exe.c:408371–408429` matches the Ref function after whitespace normalization (1,397 characters). Mechanical extraction from both switch bodies produced the ordered IDs `[2,3,6,7,8]`.
- Source-to-current branch map: state 2 calls ProcessMotion, samples time, and shares the `m_isMotionFinished` → `SetState(0)` path with state 7; current states 2/9/12 use the same attack completion behavior. State 3 clears force flags 1/2/3, clears motion freeze, advances motion, and returns alive; current state 3 preserves this order and processing while its modern corpse lifecycle retains the actor in state 10 at its final frame. State 6 advances motion and returns; current state 6 advances motion only when `m_isPc` is set, since the modern client consolidates the subclass override in the shared actor handler. State 8 advances motion, truncates `(timeGetTime()-m_stateStartTick)*0.041666668`, checks `%34`, then resolves the target and faces it. Current state 8 preserves that order and constant, uses its GID resolver, and reproduces `CRenderObject::CalcDir` from `Ref/GameActor.cpp:8831` with the source pi literal and long-double operation order. The source default delegates to base actor processing; the consolidated implementation keeps shared modern actor update integrations.
- Prologue comparison: strict `timeGetTime() > m_freezeEndTick` clears freeze and tick; vanish expiry exits before state processing; active vanish alpha follows the source formula. Current implementation retains effect-state, queued WBA, hide-fade, queued hit-wave, movement diagnostics, timestamps, and height refresh in the consolidated update.
- `tools/tests/check_pc_processstate_source.py` compares complete Ref/EXE bodies, mechanically extracted IDs/order, state branch conditions and operations, timing constants, target facing arithmetic, and current integration order. `tools/tests/check_actor_processstate_source.py` permits only the current-only state-6 label and requires its PC guard. This gate does not claim live cast/sit/death visual acceptance.

## WP2.7 combat acceptance walkthroughs — 2026-10-27

Each route below follows canonical `HighPriest.exe.c` first, checked against Ref decompilation and the current source. `tools/tests/check_am_skill_source_case.py` compares the complete Ref/EXE `Am_Skill` bodies and current generic skill/WBA branches; `check_groundskill_source.py` covers ground-skill routing; the effect and packet-specific checks are cited where relevant. The WBA log runs when the delayed hit is delivered and prints `[WBA] msg, skill, effect IDs, wave, delivery tick`.

- **Bash (5):** `GetSkillActionInfo` selects begin effect 16/motion state 2, and the source tables map attack effect `-1` / target effect 1. The action-5 special path, when target exists, builds message-96 WBA with effect IDs `-1/-1`, per-hit damage and delivery tick `now + m_attackMotion*m_motionSpeed*24`, then returns before normal state/begin-effect/impact routing. Other supported actions use generic `Am_Skill`; skill 5 targets message 131, which the receiver queues as delivered message 130. No dedicated impact effect is selected. Current source mirrors both routes. No skill-specific live packet sample is captured.
- **Double Strafe (46):** Generic `Am_Skill` clamps count to 1–36 and retains multiple hits only for actions 8/9, selects state 2/begin effect 16, launches the counted begin effect, faces the target, and schedules damage at `now + m_attackMotion*m_motionSpeed*24` (plus 192 only for jobs 1016/1420). Source tables use attack effect `-1`, target effect 1; action 9 uses message 39, and extra hits are spaced 200 ms with cumulative damage. Current route and generic checker match. No captured skill-specific action/count packet is available.
- **Fire Bolt (19):** The normal target-skill route resolves motion/begin effect, launches/aims the attack presentation, then queues the hit WBA; the source skill map selects target hit effect 49. Current `ResolveSkillAttackEffect` selects 49, and generic `Am_Skill` parity covers the surrounding WBA route. The non-monster skill-name window request (mode message 99) is legacy UI and out of scope.
- **Heal (28):** The source heal handler uses the same branch as 334/335/8001: heal amount `<200` selects effect 7, `<2000` selects 312, `<4000` selects 313, otherwise 325; it then sends damage-number message 88 with signed heal amount, color `-16711936`, kind 114. Current generated use-skill tree preserves thresholds/order. Existing `Zc_Use_Skill` source comparator covers the packet handler; no live heal packet capture is claimed.
- **Sonic Blow (136):** The source maps the attacked effect to 122. For extra hits, it first queues self message 108 with effect 122, conditionally consumes RNG on the third extra hit only when `damage/10` is nonzero, then restores the target-hit message and queues cumulative damage. Current `Am_Skill` preserves that sequence; `actor_am_skill_source_check` checks it. Effect 122's branch has its own recorded effect-handler comparison.
- **Asura Strike (271):** `GetSkillActionInfo` sets begin effect `-1` and default motion type 7; `Am_Skill` skips the initial state change, follows the generic WBA mapping, routes the target hit as message 93, then clears `effectId2` and sets the attacker to state 2. Current action map and `actor_am_skill_source_check` cover these branches. Exact skill-271 effect-table values were not separately confirmed in this walkthrough; no live replay is claimed.
- **Grimtooth (137):** Skill tables select no attacker begin/attack effect (`-1`) and target effect 132; generic `Am_Skill` delivers the WBA target effect. Current source table and `SpawnGrimToothAtk` route are source-compared in the existing Grimtooth entry/checks. No live replay is claimed.
- **Storm Gust (89):** The ground-skill route resolves destination cell and height, clears target GID, applies the mapped motion, and places effect 89 with source duration plus 10. Current `Am_Groundskill` → `ApplyGroundEffect` and target-facing mapping are covered by `check_groundskill_source.py`. No live ground-skill packet replay is claimed.
- **Sanctuary (70):** The source ground-skill map selects effect 83. The effect-83 Sanctuary handler starts `effect\\priest_sanctuary.wav` on state 0 before advancing its EZ2STR sequence; current ground mapping and effect wave table match the source route. No live unit-placement replay is claimed.

The WBA logger adds `sourceSkillId` only as current diagnostic metadata after the source-compatible 40-byte payload prefix (`attackedSpeedFactor` remains offset 32 and `effectLevel` offset 36). `Am_Skill` stamps both WBA-construction routes; `HandleAttacked` logs the final delivered message, skill ID, both effect IDs, resolved hit-wave name, and original scheduled tick. `check_wba_acceptance_trace.py` checks the metadata field order, both producer paths, the sound selection/log fields, and queue delivery gate. # `CSoundMaker::OnProcess` map ambience — 2026-10-27

- Source: `Ref/Npc.cpp:~790` and canonical `HighPriest.exe.c` VA `0x5D6850`. Complete function bodies are identical after whitespace normalization: 1,920 characters, SHA-256 `226ea97f520acf8e79603270351bcd599f232b76f05f03669c409c1508c1f849`. The runtime wave is at VA `0x5D6A53`.
- Branch order is cull (`center distance - (diagonal + range) > 130`), determine player quadrant, compute `deltaZ / deltaX`, truncate slope to signed integer, compare `abs(slope)` to integer `height / width`, project to the rectangle edge, require `edge distance - range <= 75`, require a nonempty name, then require strict `timeGetTime() > lastPlayTime + cycle`. It stores a fresh tick before attempting playback. The call uses edge-to-player X/Z offset, Y=0, integer range, integer `(range * 0.16666667)`, and source volume.

The world factory instantiates a sound maker per map sound record under `g_soundMode`; message 14 copies the RSW wave name, position, volume, width, height, range, and cycle. Cycle is multiplied by 1000 in single precision before integer conversion.
- Current counterpart is map `m_sounds` iteration in `UpdateMapAudio`, called by `CGameMode::OnUpdate`. It uses the source cull/edge comparisons, strict cycle gate and pre-play timestamp, then the relative `PlayWave` API with the source spatial/range/volume arguments. ## WP8 packet encoder trace

- `tools/index_skill_packet_emitters.py` -> `docs/skill/generated/skill_packet_emitter_callsites.tsv`: indexes 203 active direct packet-emitter callsites in `Ref/eAthena_src_2011/map/skill.c` (184 no-damage, 8 damage, 7 ground-position, 4 skill-unit update). Rows retain function, line, exact skill-argument expression, and a literal-symbol field only when the expression is a single enum-like token. Dynamic expressions are intentionally unresolved pending enclosing-branch/caller tracing; this index does not mark matrix rows verified.

- `clif_skill_nodamage`, `Ref/eAthena_src_2011/map/clif.c:4798` -> packet `0x011A` -> Ref/EXE `CGameMode::Zc_Use_Skill` -> `HandleSkillNoDamageNotify`. `tools/tests/check_clif_skill_nodamage_source.py` compares ordered wire-field writes, disguised-destination/source send branches, packet_ver 23 length, receive profile/registration, signed value decoding, and receiver dispatch. The server helper writes skill id W@2, capped value W@4, destination L@6, source-or-zero L@10, and result B@14. Client-side handling does not reflect the server's per-recipient disguise fanout; that remains transport routing. This validates one encoder/receiver route, not all per-skill packet emissions.

- `clif_skill_damage`, `Ref/eAthena_src_2011/map/clif.c:4653` -> packet `0x0114` (31 bytes, PACKETVER<3) or `0x01DE` (33 bytes, PACKETVER>=3) -> Ref/EXE `Zc_Notify_Skill` / `Zc_Notify_Skill2` -> `HandleSkillDamageNotify`. `tools/tests/check_clif_skill_damage_source.py` compares both branch field orders, packet DB lengths, current packet profiles and decoder offsets. The current decoder sign-extends the 16-bit level/division fields as Ref does; the extended branch preserves 32-bit signed damage. Server-side disguise fanout and `clif_calc_walkdelay` remain separate transport/timing operations. This validates the two encoder layouts, not per-skill caller coverage.

- `clif_skill_poseffect`, `Ref/eAthena_src_2011/map/clif.c:4831` -> packet `0x0117` (18 bytes) -> Ref/EXE `Zc_Notify_Groundskill` -> `HandleGroundSkillNotify`. `tools/tests/check_clif_skill_poseffect_source.py` checks ordered server writes, disguised-source routing, packet length/profile, exact Ref/EXE receiver equality, signed level/cell decoding and current branch anchors. The source message 99 omission is resolved in `MISMATCHES.md` as the old skill-use display UI, excluded by scope. Existing `check_groundskill_source.py` gates the delegated actor behavior. This validates the ground-notify encoder path, not all skill callers.
- `clif_skill_setunit`, `Ref/eAthena_src_2011/map/clif.c:4857` -> packet `0x011F` (16 bytes) or graffiti `0x01C9` (97 bytes) -> Ref/EXE `Zc_Skill_Entry` / `Zc_Skill_Entry2` -> `HandleSkillUnitSet` / `HandleSkillUnitSet2`. `tools/tests/check_clif_skill_setunit_source.py` checks both ordered server layouts, PACKETVER graffiti guard, packet_db lengths, exact Ref/EXE receiver body hashes, client dispatcher cases, and current trap visibility/unit creation/graffiti text paths. The graffiti marker selects the 80-byte text packet; current code copies 79 bytes and terminates the local buffer before messages 22/117.

# `CRagEffect::BLIND` levels 1–10 / effects 475–484 — 2026-10-28

- Ref `RagEffect2.cpp:18585–18630` and canonical dispatcher `HighPriest.exe.c:421864–421890` map ids 475–484 to BLIND levels 1–10. The parent copies the actor position on every frame. At state 0 it launches PP_BLIND (`0x47`) with `effect\fullb.tga` and `effect\white02.bmp`, sets NODEPTHCHECK and the parent duration, then lowers parent Y by 9. ## WP5A — `RenderHitLine` — 2026-10-28

Ref anchor `Ref/RagEffect2.cpp:16228–16490` and canonical executable body `HighPriest.exe.c:440741–441010` are identical after whitespace compaction; `tools/tests/check_hitline_render.py` extracts and asserts that full-body equality. The executable loop walks 20 adjacent history pairs, `height[j]`/`height[j+1]` and `flag[j]`/`flag[j+1]`; each point accumulates `distance*2*decay` per history count, decays by `0.69999999`, adds the positive-height term, then projects the height through sine and the GI rise angle. It adds the effect's spark X/Y/Z to both points, derives the cross-section orientation from the pair delta plus 90 degrees, fades alpha by 5 per segment, and chooses its two tint formulas using `alphaT==2`. Four ring positions use `maxHeight*cos/sin` at 0/120/240/360 degrees. The three ring intervals submit `(previousA,currentA,currentB,previousB)` with texture 0, full UVs, ONE blending, and `F1=1`, so no parent translation is applied to absolute spark points. Added the dedicated renderer and `PP_HITLINE` dispatch in `CEffectPrim::Render`; no caller IDs were routed in this checkpoint. The focused comparator and CTest `hitline_render_source_check` passed; elevated Release app and test-target builds succeeded. Coverage is unchanged at 480 routed / 213 explicit unported / 105 launched-unported. Next: effect 376 caller/helper, then 429–431.

### Renderer correction

The segment value `alphaB - segment*5` is the texture frame index and cutoff, not the output alpha. The corrected implementation skips nonpositive frame indices, keeps vertex opacity at 255, maps V to `[frame/21,(frame+1)/21]`, and matches the canonical U orientation (1→0 across the ribbon). Because the shared generic rectangle helper maps U/V along different axes, `SubmitWorldHitLineRect` submits the two triangles with explicit source UVs. `check_hitline_render.py` checks the UV tuples and ONE destination blend; direct Ref/PE and current renderer checks pass.

### RenderTeiRect overload correction

The renderer call uses the overload `RenderTeiRect(int*, short alpha, four vertices, matrix&, short PW, char TN, unsigned F1, RGB, four UV floats)` at `HighPriest.exe.c:436484`, not the overload that accepts a `TexPart` and derives V rows. Its call passes segment alpha `alphaB - v58`, TN 0, F1 1 (absolute coordinates), RGB `(255,v32,v33)`, and UVs `(0,1,0,1)`. The first renderer implementation's segment alpha and full UVs were correct; the intervening texture-frame/opacity change was reverted. The checker now verifies this precise canonical overload call and the local equivalent.

**Behavior:** State0 plays the exact executable wave `effect\wizard_meteor.wav` (VA `0x71A0D0`) relative to the listener on X/Z with Y0 and parameters250/40/1.0. State40 sends mode message135. Every call returns `ProcessEZ2STR(0,-80,0)`. The generated STR-name table selects `Meteor1.str` through `Meteor3.str` using its existing random selector.

**Evidence:** `check_effect92.py` validates PE/Ref helper equality, the inline PE case, exact audio bytes, randomized STR selector, source discrepancy and generated route. Lifecycle coverage checks frame0-only audio, exact frame40 message, result propagation and no extra RNG. Coverage audit: 605 routed / 88 explicit unported / 0 launched-but-unported. Release and parity builds pass; CTest 200/200; static audits pass. Release and OldRO executable hashes match `D5DB3EDC3EC5B166D9B0498E033FE494C4F654C47E627F2FDBDF28499AF116C2`. No live/replay acceptance is claimed.


## WP10.7 — Shared actor and skill-unit lookup — 2026-10-28

**Reference behavior:** `Ref/GameWorld.cpp:779` implements `CWorld::GetGameActorByAID` as a non-creating lookup in the active actor list; `GetSkillByAID` searches the separate skill list. WP3.2's own-AID case maps to `m_player`; other actor AIDs use the world lookup. Notify Skill source resolution retains world actor first, then skill unit. Zc_Use_Skill keeps actor-only source resolution and uses a skill-unit lookup only for its unit target cases.

**Implementation:** Replaced `ResolveNotifyEffectActor` and `ResolveCombatActor` with shared `ResolveSkillPacketActor` / `ResolveSkillPacketUnit`, and routed WP3.2 ad hoc actor and unit lookups through them. Actor lookup no longer aliases `g_session.m_gid` or `player->m_gid`, consults `m_runtimeActors`, or creates/upgrades an actor. Cast cancel retains message order84,87,83 without a runtime-map fallback. `m_runtimeActors` remains a distinct ownership/index cache; active world membership is authoritative for Ref-style packet lookups, and runtime actors enter that list via `RegisterPos`.

**Evidence:** `check_skill_packet_actor_lookup.py` checks both shared helpers, the authoritative world/unit list implementations, and 21 packet-handler bodies for raw lookups, old helpers, map fallbacks, or creation calls. Updated cast-ack/cancel, spirits, devotion, talkbox, guild-change, skill-unit and Notify Effect3 checks pass; extracted-handler test harnesses compile the production resolver bodies. Release and test builds pass; CTest 201/201; static parity audit passes. Release and OldRO SHA-256 match `DB6535BE4BA40BCF4D0E23D347DE7A34EDB1B6175856719183C71F731EB5FC2D`. Next: WP10.8 packet-replay harness.


## WP10.8 — Production-linked Bash packet replay — 2026-10-28

**Packet:** `docs/skill/replay/bash5.tsv` encodes the 31-byte `0x0114` ZC_NOTIFY_SKILL layout documented at `Ref/eAthena_src_2011/map/clif.c:4651` and confirmed by `Ref/RunningServer/packet_db.txt`. Fields specify skill5, source AID1, target AID2, tick `0x12345678`, zero source/target delay, damage100, level5, one hit, action5.

**Ref walk-through:** `Ref/GameModePacket.cpp:21262` decodes the notify-skill packet and sends message37 to the source. `Ref/GameActorMsgHandler.cpp:883` routes action5 with a resolved target and skill5 into WBA message96, no attached effects, damage100 and level5. The source plays `_enemy_hit_normal2.wav` at WBA delivery, using the target actor's default weapon hit-wave name.

**Replay:** With `RO_BUILD_SKILL_REPLAY_TESTS=ON`, the root CMake build links the actual gamemode/world/session/skill/audio/render/UI libraries. The test installs a real `CGameMode` into the mode manager, constructs the real global `CWorld`, a `CPlayer` source and registered `CGameActor` target, calls `RegisterDefaultGameModePacketHandlers`, decodes the raw scenario with production `TryReadPacket`, dispatches through `CGameModePacketRouter`, and advances the target through `ProcessWillBeAttacked` after the 24 ms delay.

**Evidence:** The production runtime log matches `WBA msg=96 skill=5 effectId1=-1 effectId2=-1 wave=_enemy_hit_normal2.wav deliveryTick=24 damage=100 level=5`; the WBA queue drains after delivery. Root replay CTest passes1/1; the standalone parity suite passes202/202. This does not verify other WP2 skills or WP8 matrix rows. Next: add Double Strafe46 and Fire Bolt19 scenarios and continue the remaining acceptance skills.

## WP10.8 replay — resource-backed multi-unit world update — 2026-10-28

Added `multi_ground_unit_resource_world_update.tsv` for two job131 units, both of which launch effect319 on their first process call. The real `alpha_down.tga` asset is copied from `Ref/GRF-Content/data/texture/effect/alpha_down.tga` into the replay working directory and loaded through `CResMgr`/`CBitmapRes`. `CWorld::UpdateGameObjects()` processes the effects appended by both units during the same live list traversal; each effect reaches a positive process count and the source texture is loaded before both units are removed with 0x0120. Ref mapping: `Ref/Skill.cpp:1361+` launches job131 effect319; Ref effect319's OnProcess2 handler (`Ref/RagEffect.cpp` near10790) calls Bottom Magnus. Implementation: `src/world/SkillUnit.cpp:382-383`, `src/world/RagEffect.cpp:25410`, and `src/world/World.cpp:5840-5855`.

The replay exposed that `ResolveCurrentMaster()` could not resolve CSkill owners: it checked the player, runtime actors, and world actors but not the skill list, so newly launched unit effects were discarded before processing. Added a `CWorld::GetSkillByAID()` lookup in `src/world/RagEffect.cpp:14718-14738`, preserving live-object validation while allowing skill-owned effects to process and stop resolving after their unit is removed. This aligns effect ownership with the existing `CSkill::LaunchLoop` path and allows safe cleanup after the owner exits. Focused CTest passes1/1; freshly built Release suite passes82/82.

# WP10.3 effect105 inline no-op

The canonical `HighPriest.exe.c` main `OnProcess` cases 105/161/198/443/489/490 only break to the shared frame-count tail; there is no corresponding primary `Ref/RagEffect.cpp` handler body. No RNG draws or resource literals occur in this branch.
